# Agent Joules build log

Purpose: the single record of toolchain facts, version deviations, scan results, test counts, per-ID filter results, probe and WSL2 notes, user actions, requirements that cannot be met and base commits (REQ-002, REQ-029, builder standing order 6). ASCII only, LF only; never a key value, a hostname or an IP. Every number carries one label: measured, user-reported, inferred or assumed.

## Toolchain check

Commands, exit codes, UTC dates and labels of the SDK, Docker, WSL2, Ollama, GPU driver and git checks (AJ-002), plus the hygiene, property and grep checks that later M0 stories run.

### AJ-001 checks (2026-10-18T16:17:53Z, measured)

| Check | Command | Exit code | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| nine headings once each | for each of the nine headings: `grep -cxF "<heading>" docs/build-log.md`; a count other than 1 prints `missing heading: <heading>` and the loop exits 1 | 0 | every count is 1 |
| ASCII only | `grep -nP "[^\x00-\x7F]" docs/build-log.md` | 1 | no match |
| LF only, story command | `grep -c $'\r' docs/build-log.md` | prints 0 | printed 0; the M0 peer reviewer measured that GNU grep 3.0 does count CR bytes when bash expands `$'\r'`, so a 0 on a CRLF file came from the executor's POSIX-sh shell not expanding `$'\r'`, not from grep; the byte count on the next row is the binding check |
| LF only, byte count | `tr -cd '\r' < docs/build-log.md` followed by `wc -c` | prints 0 | 0 CR bytes |
| no key pattern | `grep -nE "nvapi-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}" docs/build-log.md` | 1 | no match |
| negative, scratch copy: deleted heading | the heading loop on a copy without `## Test counts` | 1 | printed `missing heading: ## Test counts` |
| negative, scratch copy: non-ASCII dash | `grep -nP "[^\x00-\x7F]"` on a copy with an em-dash appended | 0 | reported the offending line number (44) |
| scratch copy: a row appended under an existing heading | one row appended under `## Secret scan`, then `grep -cxF "## Secret scan"` | prints 1 | heading count unchanged, no new heading |

Working-tree line endings (2026-10-18T16:22Z, measured with `tr -cd '\r' < <file>` and `wc -c`): `.editorconfig` has 389 CR bytes in 389 lines and `.gitignore` 482 CR bytes in 499 lines, so both are CRLF in the working tree (`git ls-files --eol` prints `i/lf w/crlf`; `core.autocrlf=true`). README.md, .gitattributes and the docs/ files are LF. The index is LF for every file. Design section 2.3 states the worktree was LF on 2026-10-18; that is not true for these two files. AJ-009 adds `* text=auto eol=lf` and re-checks the two files out as LF.

Blocked items, each naming the milestone it blocks:
- Ollama absent: the M4 local runtime (Ollama fallback, REQ-020) is blocked until the user installs Ollama and pulls the Nemotron tag that REQ-020 names (`nemotron-3-nano:30b` per prompt section 7, to be re-checked in M4); affected stories AJ-105 and AJ-107, and AJ-106 if the NIM attempt also fails; AJ-104 uses fake HTTP handlers and is not blocked; Qwen3 8B is only the optional baseline (AJ-114-T9). The M4 NIM attempt (REQ-019) is unaffected. Recorded as a user action under `## User actions` and as an unmet criterion under `## Requirements that cannot be met`; the M0 exit report carries it.
- Docker daemon running: the M1 container check (EDGE-001, EDGE-014) is not blocked.
- nvidia-smi and nvml.dll present: the M1 gated NVML integration test is not blocked.

Negative scenarios of AJ-002 (older SDK, stopped Docker daemon, missing nvidia-smi): none triggered, so no item goes under `## Requirements that cannot be met` and global.json is created by AJ-004 with 10.0.401 and is not lowered; the missing-tool rule was exercised by the Ollama row above (reason by name, milestone named, nothing invented).

## Version deviations and additions

Every package whose used version differs from the pins of design section 2.4 (package, pinned version, used version, reason) and every package added beyond the design list (REQ-002).

Decision (2026-10-18, AJ-005): stay on the prompt pins. The coordinator versions Microsoft.Extensions.AI 10.11.0, Microsoft.Agents.AI 1.25.0 and OpenTelemetry 1.20.0 are rejected (design C-01, ADR-20 row 1, open point O-01). `dotnet restore` exit 0 on all 22 pins (measured 16:28Z and 16:36Z); no NuGet audit warning; no deviation recorded. M0 adds no package beyond design section 2.4; M1 story AJ-033 adds the Options packages and records them here as additions.

| Package | Pinned version | Used version | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| none | | | no deviation in M0 |

AJ-008 (2026-10-18T16:42Z, measured): `dotnet restore` exit 0 with no missing version and no NU1901 to NU1904 audit warning, so no deviation row and no NoWarn entry were needed; the restore was a no-op and the 11 project.nuget.cache files hold no NU19 code.

Post-exit check (2026-10-18T17:59Z, measured): `dotnet restore --force` prints no NU1901 to NU1904 line; `dotnet list package --vulnerable --include-transitive` lists no vulnerable package; `dotnet list package --deprecated` flags only `xunit 2.9.3` as Legacy (alternative xunit.v3), which is the deliberate xUnit v2 plus VSTest choice of ADR-03 and ADR-04 and raises no restore warning; `dotnet list package --outdated` lists exactly the newer coordinator versions the design rejects (C-01: MEAI 10.11.0, Agent Framework 1.25.0, OpenTelemetry 1.20.0; C-03: JsonSchema.Net 9.x) plus Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting and Http 10.0.13 and xunit.runner.visualstudio 4.0.1, all informational, no pin changed.

Post-exit review (2026-10-18, from the user's code-review max run, all measured on scratch copies): `nuget.config` added at the root (single source nuget.org, `<clear/>`, package source mapping for every package) and, after the security gate, NU1900 (vulnerability feed unreachable) kept as an error unless the developer opts in with `-p:AgentJoulesOfflineAudit=true` through a conditional `WarningsNotAsErrors`; NU1507 is not downgraded because the single mapped source makes it impossible; the audit-source list is cleared in nuget.config so a user-level audit source cannot fail restore with NU1905; a vulnerable pin still fails with NU1903; NU1901 to NU1904 stay errors. `InvariantGlobalization` moved from an MSBuild property to two `RuntimeHostConfigurationOption` items (System.Globalization.Invariant, System.Globalization.PredefinedCulturesOnly) in src/Directory.Build.props and design 2.3 is amended: the property was forwarded to the analyzers and silenced CA1304 to CA1311 (a culture-sensitive `1.5.ToString("F1")` and `a.StartsWith(b)` built clean under the property and fail with CA1305 and CA1310 without it); the Cli runtimeconfig still carries `System.Globalization.Invariant: true`. No package or version changed.

Scan of record for AJ-011: `git grep fallback` (gitleaks not installed), exit code 1 (no match), verdict clean, measured first at commit 8635b32 (2026-10-18T16:48:49Z) and re-run at commit 71a1cf4 (2026-10-18T17:07:24Z) after the AJ-008, AJ-009 and AJ-010 commits that touched docs/ and tests/. Rule (broadened on the security reviewer's finding): the scan is re-run on the working tree before each commit until M7 (tracked, untracked and history greps, so the content about to be committed is covered), not only before commits that touch docs/ or config/, because test fixtures under tests/ and src/ are the likelier leak; the latest exit code is the one that counts; each re-run is appended as the next row of the table above, the M0 exit re-run included; future pattern re-runs use `-l` instead of `-n` so a hit prints only the file name. Known limits of the fallback against gitleaks: it cannot catch keys that are not in the current environment, credentials without the `nvapi-` prefix and without a registered value, encoded or split values, dangling objects, or high-entropy strings of unknown shape. Amended 2026-10-18 (post-exit review): the exact-value greps pass the value on stdin, `printf '%s\n' "${!NAME}" | git grep -qF -f -` (also with `--untracked` and over `$(git rev-list --all)`), never as a command-line argument, because an argument is visible in process listings and git traces while git runs; an empty value makes git grep exit 128, so the empty-variable guard stays.

## Probe and WSL2 notes

Results of `probe` runs and of the Linux and WSL2 execution checks (M1 and later).

Pre-measurement 2026-10-19T09:00Z (measured, for AJ-036 and AJ-037): `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=driver_version,name,power.limit --format=csv,noheader` printed `616.92, NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3080 Ti Laptop GPU, [N/A]` (power.limit not reported by nvidia-smi in this mode); `docker manifest inspect mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0` exit 0 (the tag exists); `actionlint` is on PATH. WSL2: Ubuntu-22.04 has .NET SDKs 8.0.414 and 9.0.203 but no 10.0.4xx, so `dotnet` fails against the repository's global.json (10.0.401); Ubuntu has no dotnet; kali-linux has SDK 6.0.400 only; all three distros have `nvidia-smi` and `/usr/lib/wsl/lib/libnvidia-ml.so.1`. Conclusion for AJ-036-T4 (inferred): the WSL2 probe cannot run with the distro SDKs as installed; the story allows "not run: <reason>", and the library path confirms the design 5.6 inference that WSL2 exposes NVML under `/usr/lib/wsl/lib`.

Laptop NVML facts from the gated integration test (measured 2026-10-19T10:42Z and 10:43Z, AJ-023): name `NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3080 Ti Laptop GPU`; driver `616.92`; `nvmlDeviceGetTotalEnergyConsumption` supported (rc 0), so the counter method is available on this GPU (design R-01 resolved; the prompt's UNVERIFIED row is now measured); `nvmlDeviceGetCurrentClocksEventReasons` supported (rc 0), so EDGE-013 throttle detection has data on this laptop and the AJ-028 criterion 7 sentence for the M4 methodology is "throttle reasons are supported on this GPU" (the unsupported sentence is not needed); MIG `not-supported` (rc 3); enforced power limit 91.210 W at 10:42Z and 88.554 W at 10:43Z (also 75.000 and 87.699 W earlier today, 85.059 W on 2026-10-18 by NVML, 94.62 W on 2026-10-17 by nvidia-smi): the limit moves with the laptop power mode within a minute, so no document quotes a fixed limit and the committed probe (AJ-037) is the authority at run time (design C-11). Two further limit readings: 93.711 W (code-review gate) and 95.283 W at 10:45Z (spec gate). AJ-028 criterion 7 is satisfied by this record: the throttle API is supported, and the AJ-028 Specs scenario reads this paragraph.

Probe output on the laptop (measured 2026-10-19, AJ-035): `probe` printed the ten lines: GPU name `NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3080 Ti Laptop GPU`, GPU UUID `GPU-7687512b...` (the first eight characters after the prefix; the rest not recorded), Driver version `616.92`, Compute capability `8.6`, VBIOS version `94.03.1b.00.3a`, Power limit W `87.867`, MIG mode `not-supported`, Energy counter supported `true`, Method `counter`, Sample rate Hz `10`; `probe --json` printed the eleven-key object with powerLimitW 87.619 on the second run; both exit 0 with empty stderr. The enforced power limit moved between two consecutive runs (87.867 W, then 87.619 W); the earlier readings in this section are 94.62 W (nvidia-smi, 2026-10-17), 85.059 W (NVML, 2026-10-18) and 75.000, 87.699, 91.210, 88.554, 93.711 and 95.283 W (NVML, 2026-10-19). A probe value is therefore a snapshot at its instant; AJ-037 commits the probe file as the authority at run time (design C-11).

2026-10-19 (AJ-036): operational checks, one line each.

docker info exit 0 at 20:04:32Z; docker manifest inspect mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0 exit 0 at 20:04:34Z (the inferred tag exists; no replacement tag needed).

EDGE-001 container command run from Git Bash at the repo root 20:04:39Z to 20:05:11Z, exit 0 in 32 s, no --gpus flag: `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1 docker run --rm -e DOTNET_NOLOGO=1 -e DOTNET_CLI_TELEMETRY_OPTOUT=1 -v "$(pwd -W)":/src -w /src mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0 dotnet run --project src/AgentJoules.Cli -- probe --json` printed only the eleven-key JSON with gpuPresent false and every other value null (stderr 0 bytes, no SDK banner; pwd -W printed D:/src/github/NvidiaSDKProjects/hackathon-nebius-nvidia-2026/agent-joules).

host repair after the container run (design R-10): dotnet restore exit 0 at 20:05:15Z (4 of 11 projects up to date, seven re-restored), dotnet build -warnaserror -tl:off exit 0 at 20:05:22Z with 0 warnings.

WSL2 probe not run: no .NET SDK 10.0.4xx in any WSL2 distro (Ubuntu-22.04, the default, has 8.0.414 and 9.0.203; Ubuntu has no dotnet; kali-linux has 6.0.400), so global.json 10.0.401 cannot be satisfied; measured 20:05:25Z to 20:06:03Z; nvidia-smi inside the default distro sees the NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3080 Ti Laptop GPU, so the proposed third candidate /usr/lib/wsl/lib/libnvidia-ml.so.1 (criterion 6) stays unexercised and is not applied.

### AJ-001 Create the build log with fixed sections (REQ-002)

- Shipped: docs/build-log.md with the nine fixed headings, a tenth heading `## Story log`, a one-line purpose under each, and the AJ-001 check rows under `## Toolchain check`.
- Decisions: the tenth heading `## Story log` is the home of the per-story entries that docs/plan/milestone-session-prompt.md section 4 step 6 requires; none of the nine fits them. This is a deviation from the story's heading list and is declared here. Sub-agent tokens in every story entry count the implementation and gate calls; the docs call that writes the entry is excluded there and added to the milestone total at the exit.
- Deviations: the story's LF check `grep -c $'\r'` printed 0 even on CRLF files when run through the executor's POSIX-sh shell, which does not expand `$'\r'` (grep itself counts CR when bash expands it, measured by the M0 peer reviewer), so a byte count is logged beside it as the binding check. Design section 2.3 claims an LF working tree; `.editorconfig` and `.gitignore` are CRLF in the working tree (measured), to be fixed by AJ-009.

### AJ-004 Pin the SDK and share the build properties (REQ-001)

- Shipped: `global.json`, `Directory.Build.props`, `src/Directory.Build.props` (GetPathOfFileAbove import, InvariantGlobalization), `tests/Directory.Build.props` (import, IsTestProject, NoWarn, shared test packages), `.editorconfig` with the one in-place change and the appended analyzer and LF sections.
- Decisions: the shared test PackageReference ItemGroup is conditioned on `'$(MSBuildProjectName)' != 'AgentJoules.TestSupport'` so the design's "except TestSupport" holds for packages as well as for IsTestProject; the appended `.editorconfig` block starts with a comment and an explicit `[*.cs]` header so the diagnostics are not scoped by the file's last section; `.editorconfig` keeps its CRLF working-tree endings (git normalises on commit) and keeps `insert_final_newline = false` for `[*.cs]`, so `dotnet format` strips the trailing newline of every .cs file.
- Deviations: none from the story. Three MINOR code-review notes accepted without change: (1) `$(NoWarn);CS1591` drops the SDK default `1701;1702` (binding-redirect warnings that .NET 10 does not emit; the reviewer's scratch build with the pinned packages gave 0 warnings); (2) `end_of_line = lf` is only safe after AJ-009 adds `* text=auto eol=lf`, so no checkout, stash or restore happens between this commit and AJ-009; (3) NU1901 to NU1904 audit warnings become errors under TreatWarningsAsErrors, accepted on purpose because the AJ-008 rule resolves them by version and never by NoWarn.
- Gates: pooled with AJ-003 (see that entry); code-review-gatekeeper APPROVED with the three MINOR notes above; security-specialist APPROVED; spec-compliance-reviewer APPROVED (criteria 1 to 6 PASS; the two test-project checks it deferred are measured in the table above).

### AJ-010 Redact key values in any text output (REQ-029)

- Shipped: `src/AgentJoules.Core/Security/SecretRedactor.cs` (Register, Redact, the `nvapi-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{4,}` pattern as a generated regex, longest-first registered values, the six-character reveal with the short-value rule, superseded by the follow-up entry below), `src/AgentJoules.Cli/Output/RedactingTextWriter.cs` (line-buffered TextWriter that redacts before anything reaches the inner writer; Flush and Dispose write the remainder), `tests/AgentJoules.UnitTests/RedactionTests.cs` (19 facts at that commit, 21 after the follow-up, trait Spec=REQ-029), `tests/AgentJoules.Specs/Features/Configuration/AJ-010-redact-key-values.feature` (the story's seven scenarios verbatim) and `tests/AgentJoules.Specs/Steps/ConfigurationSteps.cs`. Console wiring is AJ-034 (M1).
- Decisions: the regex runs before the registered values (a registered value such as `-` would otherwise split the `nvapi-` prefix and leak the suffix; found by the code review, fixed and guarded by a test); registered values are also registered trimmed, so a trailing CR from a CRLF file cannot leak the bare key; the writer owns and disposes its inner writer, passes FormatProvider and NewLine through, and is documented as not thread-safe (AJ-034 wraps it with TextWriter.Synchronized, one instance per stream); the first version of this commit kept the story's six-character reveal; the M0 peer review cited design-review N-09 (every other secret becomes `****`), so design 3.2 is the authority, the story text was superseded and the AJ-010 follow-up commit masks every registered value as `****` (entry below).
- Deviations: one step pattern in ConfigurationSteps.cs carried `^...$` anchors because Reqnroll parsed it as a Cucumber expression otherwise; the follow-up commit replaced that binding, so no binding is anchored now. Noted for AJ-034, not applied: a span-merging implementation would also cover a registered value that contains `nvapi-` followed by a character outside the key charset (real NVIDIA keys use only the charset, so it cannot trigger today); a registered value containing `*` breaks idempotence of the mask (output only grows, nothing leaks); the security reviewer's proposal to reveal nothing for values shorter than 16 characters and to lock the writer, both declined for M0 because they change the story's rule or are handled by the M1 wiring.
- Gates: code-review-gatekeeper round 1 CHANGES_REQUIRED (MAJOR: regex ordering leak; MAJOR: untested boundaries; MINOR: Dispose try/finally, FormatProvider and NewLine, trimmed registration, deterministic tie-break, Register inside the Given step, StartsWith guard), all applied; round 2 APPROVED (both MAJORs verified fixed and tested; MINORs recorded above); security-specialist APPROVED (no partial mask reaches the inner writer, regex linear, secrets held privately, no literal key; three LOW notes recorded above); spec-compliance-reviewer APPROVED (criteria 1 to 6 PASS, criterion 7 DEFERRED to AJ-034 by the story; the feature file equals the story's Gherkin byte for byte).
- Velocity: estimate 1.75 h; wall-clock 16:48Z (red phase start) to 17:06Z (entry written), 18 min, commit follows; sub-agent tokens 661,237 (csharp-expert 85,506 + 101,002 + 114,973; gates 116,390 + 98,701 + 102,972 + 41,693).

### AJ-020 Provide the NVML and time test doubles (REQ-003, REQ-004)

- Shipped: `src/AgentJoules.Nvml/INvmlApi.cs` (the 14 members: Init, Shutdown, GetHandleByIndex, GetPowerUsage, GetTotalEnergyConsumption, GetClocksEventReasons, GetMigMode, GetCount, GetName, GetUuid, GetVbios, GetComputeCapability, GetPowerLimit, GetDriverVersion, all returning `NvmlReturn`); `src/AgentJoules.Nvml/Interop/NvmlReturn.cs` (Success 0, Uninitialized 1, InvalidArgument 2, NotSupported 3, values from nvml.h, URL cited in the XML summary, `https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/nvml-api/api/group__nvmlDeviceEnums.html` (the `/api/` path form; the earlier `group__nvmlReturn.html` link returned 404, measured by the reviewer)); `INvmlApi.cs` carries a remarks block: members may throw only DllNotFoundException, EntryPointNotFoundException or BadImageFormatException, and handles are borrowed and valid only between Init and the matching Shutdown; `tests/AgentJoules.TestSupport/ScriptedNvmlApi.cs` (power as a function of fake time (`WattsAt`, `ConstantPowerWatts` constant setter); the counter as the millijoule integral of the power actually scripted, segmented at every script change (the old script's integral is folded into a base; exact for constants, trapezoid over a 1 ms grid with a sub-ms tail otherwise) plus `CounterOffsetMillijoules` (default 123456789, since the real counter counts since driver load) and a never-decreasing guard; `MissingLibrary` makes all 14 members throw DllNotFoundException; `FaultFor` per-member fault hook (any exception from any call, for AJ-022); `CounterSupported` and `CounterReturn`, `CounterReturnForRead`, `PowerReturnForRead` (a scripted non-Success code leaves the out value 0), `PowerCallCount`, `PowerReadCount`, `CounterCallCount`, `CounterReadCount`, `CounterReads`, `ThrowOnPowerReadNumber` (a source-level fault outside the real binding's contract); reference-counted Init and Shutdown like NVML, handles bound to the session generation (`(generation << 16) | (index + 1)`; `FirstDeviceHandle` is the first session's first handle; a stale handle returns InvalidArgument); - Security mitigation note (final wording from the security gate, 2026-10-19; also placed in design 5.6):

  Linux and WSL2 (inferred for the cloudgpu VM; measured in Docker on the laptop): the resolver passes `libnvidia-ml.so.1`, then `libnvidia-ml.so`, to `NativeLibrary.TryLoad(name, out h)`, which hands the soname to `dlopen` unchanged. The dynamic loader then searches `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, the executable's `DT_RUNPATH`, `/etc/ld.so.cache` (on WSL2 this includes `/usr/lib/wsl/lib` through `/etc/ld.so.conf.d`), then `/lib` and `/usr/lib`. The .NET host's native-library folders, including the application folder of a self-contained publish, are not probed. When neither soname loads, the resolver throws `DllNotFoundException`, so the runtime's default search never runs. Anyone who can set `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` can already inject code through `LD_PRELOAD` or `DOTNET_STARTUP_HOOKS`, so no privilege boundary is crossed. Operating rules: run the CLI as an unprivileged user, never as root; never run it with an `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` that contains an empty entry or `.`, because `dlopen` reads those as the current directory; keep `artifacts/linux-x64` on the VM writable only by the deploying user.

  Windows (measured 2026-10-19): `nvml.dll` is loaded by its full path under `Environment.SystemDirectory`. Its static imports are `KERNEL32.dll` and `ADVAPI32.dll` only, both KnownDLLs, so its dependencies never come from the current directory or PATH. The NVSMI fallback is tried only when `ProgramW6432` is a fully qualified path and the System32 copy fails to load. Anyone who controls the environment can already run code in the process.
- Carried forward: AJ-022 builds the `Unavailable` reason from fixed text naming `nvml.dll` or `libnvidia-ml.so.1`, never from the exception message, because the resolver's message lists the full load paths including whatever `ProgramW6432` held (security advisory); the story that adds kernel32 imports to this assembly (`PowerSourceDetector`, design 3.1) must route them to System32 full paths too, since `Resolve` returns zero for other names and the runtime default search probes the host folders first (security advisory); the deprecated-export reader path is proven at the reader level only, the laptop driver has the new export (code-review and spec remark); `Resolve`'s throw path, the NVSMI branch and the Linux branch have not run on the laptop host itself, the Linux branch is measured in Docker (above).

one private lock taken after the dispose check and re-checked inside it; Interlocked dispose flag; `Dispose` takes the lock and calls Shutdown exactly once, also from two threads, and lets a Shutdown exception propagate; the three Read members throw `ObjectDisposedException` after Dispose while `Identity` and the counter properties stay readable; a non-Success read throws `InvalidOperationException` naming the member and the integer code, never returning 0; `ReadEnergyMillijoules` returns null without re-querying when the counter is unsupported; `ReadThrottleReasons` returns `Unsupported` on NotSupported and throws on any other non-Success code); the three Read members of `IGpuTelemetrySource` carry `<exception>` docs for `ObjectDisposedException` and `InvalidOperationException`. `src/AgentJoules.Nvml/NvmlGpuTelemetrySourceFactory.cs` (`Open(index)`: a negative index gives `no device at index <n>` before any call; `nvmlInit_v2 returned <rc>`; count then `no device at index <n>`; `<export name> returned <rc>` for a failed handle, name, UUID, driver-version or power-limit query; VBIOS and compute capability null on failure; MIG rc 3 and any other non-Success give `not-supported`, rc 0 gives `disabled` for current 0 and `enabled` otherwise; exactly one `nvmlDeviceGetTotalEnergyConsumption` read decides the counter support (the single startup probe of REQ-004; AJ-027 reads the property); `DllNotFoundException`, `EntryPointNotFoundException` and `BadImageFormatException` from any call give `Unavailable` with the fixed text `NVML library not found: nvml.dll (<exception type name>)` on Windows and `NVML library not found: libnvidia-ml.so.1 (...)` on every other platform, never the exception message; Shutdown is attempted once after a successful Init on every failure path with its own exception swallowed, so the session is never left initialised; no exception escapes Open); `Open` wraps everything after a successful Init in try/finally with a handed-off flag so any exception type shuts the session down exactly once (only the three native types map to Unavailable, documented in a remarks block); `Dispose` swallows the three native exception types from Shutdown and still marks the source disposed; integers in reasons and messages use `string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, ...)`; the constructor is internal. Tests: about 38 methods appended to `tests/AgentJoules.UnitTests/NvmlBindingTests.cs` incl. a nested `ReturnCodeOverrideApi : INvmlApi` wrapper that overrides one member's return code, `Reads_are_serialized_under_contention` (8 threads x 100 reads, 800 recorded, `MaxConcurrentReads` 1), `Mixed_reads_share_the_same_lock`, a reflection check that no field of the source is a SafeHandle; `tests/AgentJoules.Specs/Features/Metering/AJ-022-open-dispose-device.feature` (seven scenarios verbatim); steps in `Steps/MeteringSteps.cs`; `FactoryPlatform`, `OpenResult`, `Source` in `Support/ScenarioWorld.cs` (Dispose disposes the source first). Fix-round tests: `A_read_racing_Dispose_throws_only_ObjectDisposedException` (100 rounds, 4 readers cycling the three reads against one disposer; the builder deleted the three in-lock re-checks once and the test failed, restored byte-identical), `Reason_and_message_integers_are_formatted_with_the_invariant_culture` (sv-SE; asserts ASCII hyphens in `no device at index -1`, `nvmlInit_v2 returned -7`, `counter-not-supported: -7`), `A_fault_outside_the_contract_propagates_but_the_session_is_shut_down_once`, `Dispose_swallows_a_native_load_exception_from_Shutdown_and_still_disposes`, `Open_calls_exactly_the_members_in_OpenMembers`, and `ReadEnergyMillijoules_returns_the_absolute_counter_not_a_delta_since_Open` (default offset 123456789, asserts 123656789).

### AJ-025 Integrate power samples into joules (REQ-004)

- Shipped: `src/AgentJoules.Core/Energy/PowerSample.cs` (`readonly record struct PowerSample(double Seconds, double Watts, ThrottleReasons Reasons)`; seconds since meter start) and `PowerIntegrator.cs` (static; `IntegrateJoules`, `AverageWatts` and `PeakWatts` with `ReadOnlySpan<PowerSample>` and `IReadOnlyList<PowerSample>` overloads; the integral is the trapezoid between consecutive samples plus the first sample's watts held from the window start to its timestamp and the last sample's watts held from its timestamp to the window stop, from the actual timestamps and never a nominal interval (design 3.2); validation order: the window first (NaN or infinite bounds, stop <= start throw ArgumentException naming the window parameter), then every sample (non-finite timestamp, non-finite or negative watts, a sample before start or after stop, a timestamp not strictly increasing: ArgumentException whose message reads `The sample at index N ...` with the index formatted in the invariant culture), then fewer than two samples returns null so the caller labels the value not-reported, never 0; `AverageWatts` is the integral over the window duration (null when the integral is null), `PeakWatts(samples, windowStartSeconds, windowStopSeconds)` the maximum watts, validated like the integral and null below two samples; the list overloads view arrays and `List<T>` in place through `CollectionsMarshal.AsSpan` and copy any other list once, the span path allocates nothing except on the throw path); fix round: `PeakWatts(samples, windowStartSeconds, windowStopSeconds)` validates like the integral and returns null below two samples (one validated contract for the three methods); `IntegrateJoules` and `PeakWatts` validate and accumulate in one pass (each element read once into a local) so a caller mutating an in-place-viewed list cannot slip an unchecked value past validation; `Validate` rejects a non-finite window duration; a non-finite sum or average throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` naming `samples`; the accumulator starts at 0.0 so all -0.0 watts give +0.0; every sample rejection carries `ParamName` `samples`; the `IReadOnlyList` overloads document the in-place view of arrays and `List<T>`, the no-mutation rule, the `Count` contract and the `ArgumentNullException`; `PeakWatts` normalises an all -0.0 input to +0.0 like the integral; 1e308 W over 10 s), the ParamName test, the -0.0 test, the nominal trapezoid 42.5 J asserted outside the band, a theory over `PowerSample[]`, `List<PowerSample>` and `ReadOnlyCollection<PowerSample>` for the three methods, null-list tests; tolerance assertions `Assert.Equal(expected, actual, 1e-9)`; the BDD rejection step asserts `index` for an integration failure; trait Spec=REQ-004; AJ-027 and AJ-028 add to this class): the 21-sample 50 W case with timestamps built as `i / 10.0` (100 J to 9 digits over 0 to 2 s) and as twenty accumulated 0.1 s steps (100 J within 1 percent, the window closed at the last sample's own timestamp because the accumulation overshoots 2.0 by one ulp and the bounds are strict), the 40 to 60 W ramp sampled at 0.0, 0.05, 0.40, 1.00, 1.75, 2.0 s (100 J within 1 percent and exact to 1e-9 relative), two 50 W samples at 0.2 and 0.8 s over 0 to 1 s giving 50 J to 9 digits, the `power = 100 x t` samples at 0, 0.1, 0.5, 0.6, 1.0 s giving 50 J within 1 percent while the nominal-interval sum (watts times 0.25 s per gap) is not within 1 percent, the rejections (equal and decreasing timestamps, a sample before start and after stop, stop <= start, NaN watts, infinite timestamp, negative watts) with `index N` in the message, a single or no sample returning null, AverageWatts 50 W for 100 J over 2 s, PeakWatts; `tests/AgentJoules.Specs/Features/Metering/AJ-025-integrate-power.feature` (seven scenarios verbatim); steps in `Steps/MeteringSteps.cs` (anchored regexes; the step text `power = 100 W/s x t` contains a slash); integration state in `Support/ScenarioWorld.cs`.

- Decisions and deviations: strict window bounds without an epsilon (a sample outside the window throws even when it is the only sample, as scenario 7 requires; the meter of AJ-029 takes its edge samples at the exact open and close instants so no production sample falls outside); the accumulated-timestamp variant of criterion 1 closes its window at the last sample's own timestamp (twenty additions of 0.1 give 2.0000000000000004, measured); criterion 3's "exactly 50 J" is asserted to 9 decimal digits because 0.8 - 0.2 is not exact in binary floating point; the sample messages carry `ParamName` `samples` through a helper that takes the name as a parameter (the code-review gate measured that only a literal name inside a helper without that parameter triggers CA2208; the earlier rationale was wrong); equal timestamps: design 5.3 says the integrator asserts non-decreasing timestamps while story criterion 5 says equal timestamps throw; the build follows the story (strictly increasing) and the conflict is resolved at the meter: AJ-029's buffer never holds two samples at one instant (the immediate open or close sample supersedes a timer tick taken at the same timestamp), takes the timestamp and appends under one lock, and selects each window's slice by timestamp so a loop sample after the close instant is excluded (code-review and spec gates, carried to AJ-029); the `IReadOnlyList` overloads read arrays and `List<T>` in place; AJ-029 integrates a window by slicing `CollectionsMarshal.AsSpan(buffer)` under its lock, or by copying the window's slice under the lock and integrating outside it, and never lets a span over the buffer outlive the lock (security gate, carried to AJ-029); `PeakWatts` validates like the other two methods and returns null below two samples, so AJ-026's `ReadingCalculator` calls the three helpers and labels null results not-reported with reason insufficient-samples (spec and security gates); negative watts are rejected although NVML cannot produce them (unsigned milliwatts), so the integrator is not reusable on power-minus-baseline values; the dashboard estimate (design 3.10) reuses only the rule (spec gate); `AverageWatts` also throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException when the division is not finite (builder's guard, no test covers it); the `PeakWatts` signature changed from `(samples)` to `(samples, start, stop)` before any consumer existed; per-sample checks run in a fixed order (timestamp finite, watts finite, watts non-negative, inside the window, strictly increasing); the integrator takes the timestamps it is given, the DoD's `TimeProvider.GetTimestamp()` rule binds the meter (AJ-029); tooling note: the Bash tool's heredocs break on apostrophes here, the builder wrote the Core files with the Write tool.

`src/AgentJoules.Nvml/AssemblyAttributes.cs` adds `[assembly: InternalsVisibleTo("AgentJoules.Specs")]` so the Specs project binds scenario 3 to the internal `ClocksEventReader`; `NvmlGpuTelemetrySource.ReadThrottleReasons` unchanged (it already maps NotSupported to `Unsupported`, AJ-022); Criterion 2 header check (builder, 2026-10-19, measured): the reference page `https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/nvml-api/api/group__nvmlClocksEventReasons.html` returned HTTP 200 but lists macro names only, so the values were taken from the go-nvml copy of `nvml.h` (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA/go-nvml/main/gen/nvml/nvml.h`, HTTP 200, NVML_API_VERSION 13): GpuIdle 0x1, ApplicationsClocksSetting 0x2, SwPowerCap 0x4, HwSlowdown 0x8, SyncBoost 0x10, SwThermalSlowdown 0x20, HwThermalSlowdown 0x40, HwPowerBrakeSlowdown 0x80, all eight equal to the design's inferred values; the header defines HwSlowdown, HwThermalSlowdown and HwPowerBrakeSlowdown only under the `nvmlClocksThrottleReason` prefix and the other five under `nvmlClocksEventReason`; the header also defines 0x100 DisplayClockSetting, 0x200 BoardLimit and 0x400 Reliability, which the design does not map and which never set the flag (tested).

After the code-review gate the Specs scenarios and four unit cases drive the bits through `ScriptedNvmlApi.ClockEventReasons` and the real `NvmlGpuTelemetrySource.ReadThrottleReasons()` per sample, and the unsupported case through `ClockEventReturn = NotSupported`, so EDGE-013's acceptance (a fake NVML reporting a power-cap reason) is met literally; `ThrottleReasons.ReadingNames(bool everyReadSupported)` implements the coverage rule (null when any sample was unsupported and nothing was active, an empty list only when every sample was supported and none carried a bit, the names otherwise) and is the single place for the null-versus-empty rule; the fold over a window's samples lives in `ReadingCalculator`, so the Gpu namespace no longer depends on Energy; `AllNames` is built once. Coverage rule (security gate, dated 2026-10-19): `throttleReasons` is the list of names when any sample carries a throttling bit, an empty list only when every sample in the window was supported and none carried one, and null when any sample was unsupported and nothing was active, so a negative claim needs full coverage while a positive one needs a single sample; the three unmapped header bits (DisplayClockSetting 0x100, BoardLimit 0x200, Reliability 0x400) never set the flag pending open decision D-13; `InternalsVisibleTo("AgentJoules.Specs")` is a compile-time grant on unsigned assemblies and not a security boundary (a public export-injection seam would widen the shipped API), any future native call from Specs must sit behind the integration gate. The class-level REQ-004 trait on PowerIntegrationTests makes the REQ-004 count rise by 74 with this story's EDGE-013 cases (measured by the spec gate), without new REQ-004 work.

- Decisions (2026-10-19): the content root under the harness is CliHostOverrides.WorkingDirectory (already in the design record), so no new member; the save happens before any stdout write, so a failed save leaves stdout empty; the `saved:` line goes to stderr so stdout stays pure; scenario 8's "Given the committed probe driverVersion differs" is the gate Given and the comparison runs in When; criterion 2's trimmed comparison uses the first nvidia-smi line (identical on a single-GPU laptop); the process start for scenario 3 lives in the steps because CliChildProcess disposes its workspace before the saved file could be read; three format fixes (an auto property in the steps, two [GeneratedRegex] methods in the tests making the class partial, a collection expression); the 16-character echo cap is implemented in Cli (a Cli-local Echo, because TextEcho is internal to Core). Fix round 1 decision (2026-10-19): the save writes `results/.probe-<ts>.tmp` with CreateNew and then moves it onto the final name without overwrite, so a dangling link at the target cannot redirect the write and a crash never leaves a partial file under the final name; a `results` junction is followed and documented, not rejected; every I/O failure in Save maps to `file exists: results/<name>` or `cannot write results/<name>: <ExceptionType>` so no absolute path reaches stderr; the second line of defence (name-format and containment checks inside Save) is unreachable from the CLI and stays untested rather than adding an InternalsVisibleTo to the Cli. Decision (2026-10-20): the caught IOException or UnauthorizedAccessException is not attached as the inner exception of the `file exists` and `cannot write` errors: the builder measured that CommandHost logs a failed command at Debug level through OneLineConsoleFormatter with exception.ToString(), which printed the framework message with the absolute path on stderr when the inner exception was attached; the diagnostic loses the framework reason and keeps stderr free of paths (test A_failed_save_keeps_the_absolute_path_off_stderr_even_when_the_debug_log_prints_the_exception_chain with Logging:LogLevel:Default=Debug). CliHarness.RunTokensAsync(string[]) and a CliHarnessRun string[] constructor are the non-splitting entry for arguments that carry whitespace or line terminators; OffsetClock (TestSupport) is a TimeProvider whose GetUtcNow returns 2026-10-19T14:30:15+05:00.

- Decisions: `TokenCounts` lives in Core without a Microsoft.Extensions.AI reference (the decorator passes the two numeric counts, so no provider object reaches Core and `AdditionalCounts` is never read, a security recommendation of AJ-041); the cumulative-usage design test (updates carrying 11/0, 11/1, 11/2) yields 11 and 2; the flag is added to the energy flags of the reading, never in their place; the M1 vocabulary tests were updated because the new member made 128 a valid flag; a test types `NotConfiguredCostCalculator` concretely because analyzer CA1859 rejects the interface-typed variable. Open point raised by the builder for the gates: a throwing `ICostCalculator.Compute` (or `StepReading` constructor) inside the decorator's finally would mask the call's own exception, like the meter-window case AJ-041 closed; the only M2 implementation never throws. Fix round: the cost computation, the `StepReading` construction and `Sink.Publish` run in one guarded block, so after a failed call a failure there is attached under `Exception.Data[MeteredChatClient.ReadingFailureKey]` ("AgentJoules.ReadingFailure") and the call's own exception reaches the caller, while after a successful call the failure surfaces (security MEDIUM, measured: a throwing calculator had replaced an inner TimeoutException); streaming usage replaces per count (a non-null `InputTokenCount` or `OutputTokenCount` replaces that count, a null leaves the earlier value), so a trailing usage chunk with both counts null no longer wipes measured counts or the max-tokens flag, and the real OpenAI `include_usage` shape (a final text-less usage update) is captured with TTFT unchanged; `EnergyReading` rejects the step flag `max-tokens-exceeded` through `EnergyShapeRules.RequireNoStepFlags` (the doc claim is now enforced); `ICostCalculator` documents thread-safety and that its wall-seconds argument is the call's latency until AJ-045 decides which figure the cloudgpu hourly price uses; `MeteredCallsContext.Cost` is get-only.

the energy window, startUtc and endUtc span the whole call including waits while latencyMs and TTFT exclude them; cost wall seconds include the waits (the time a rented GPU is billed for; orchestrator decision closing the AJ-042 carry); `RetriesExhaustedException` carries no inner exception and no provider text because a 429 or 401 body can echo the key (security rule from AJ-044); the Gherkin scenario "fails with 429 after one update" injects a captured real-SDK 429 exception through `FailAfterUpdatesChatClient` because an HTTP status cannot follow a body that has started (the fake endpoint's mid-stream cut is an IOException classified `incomplete`, EDGE-016, tested separately); the exact-time driver replaces the step driver for the retry tests because a step could advance the clock past a wait and break the exact 2000/100 assertions; fix round: `RetryOptions` gains `MaxRetryAfter` (60 s; build.nvidia.com limits per minute) and `MaxTotalWait` (120 s per call); a valid `Retry-After` above the ceiling, a digit string too large for a long, or a wait that would push the call's summed wait past the budget throws `RetriesExhaustedException(attemptsMade, 429)` at once instead of waiting or falling back to jitter (security MEDIUM: a one-day header held the serialized gate for 96 h of fake time against an always-429 endpoint; the one-day special case is gone; a past HTTP date still falls back to the computed jitter); validation rejects `MaxDelay` above `MaxRetryAfter`, non-positive ceilings and a non-finite `Factor`, and `RetriesExhaustedException` rejects a status outside 100 to 599; the ledger records the time actually waited, measured on the clock in a finally, and `RetryLedger.AddWait` records a cut-short wait without counting a retry, so latency = elapsed minus wait holds for a call cancelled during a wait (security LOW: latencyMs 30,000 with waitMs 0 had been measured); `RateLimitRetryChatClient.GetService(typeof(RetryLedger))` returns its ledger, the 4-argument `MeteredChatClient` constructor throws `ArgumentException` on a different instance (the builder overload at `Build()`), and the 3-argument constructor adopts the discovered ledger or creates its own, so a misassembled pipeline cannot silently report retries 0 (code-review MINOR); the key-echo test has a positive control (the SDK's own exception carries the key), checks every 6-character window and has a streaming variant; the two `ThrowsAnyAsync<Exception>` asserts are specific (`ClientResultException` with an inner `HttpRequestException`; `IOException`); the retried-stream-yields-once test and the parser pins (three HTTP-date formats, the 60/61 s boundary, a date beyond the ceiling, option rejections) are added; `A_timeout_is_attempted_once` deliberately waits about 1 s of wall clock because the SDK NetworkTimeout cannot run on a fake clock (documented); the energy window spanning the waits is accepted (design 3.3 item 2; the local runtimes send no 429; a methodology note follows in M4).

`YieldOutputAsync` runs outside the failure-recording block so a failed yield is a graph fault; the process-wide `ClockGate` lock in `FakeChatClient` is removed (`FakeTimeProvider` 10.11.0 locks `GetUtcNow`, `GetTimestamp` and `Advance` internally; the rendezvous test passed three of three without it); the provider exception stays as `InnerException` on `StepFailedException` with the class doc forbidding printing it outside Debug logging, pinned by a test that `ErrorLine.Format` contains no 6-character window of a runtime-built key placed in the inner message (the policy differs from `RetriesExhaustedException`, which keeps no inner exception because it is itself the printed failure; recorded in the design); reasoning updates are excluded from the folded text and still count for TTFT (test, 299 to 301 ms); the first failure event wins when both branches fail (documented); the quiescence test also asserts the Writer reading is `ok`; `FanOutFanInContext` is `IAsyncDisposable` with an async `[AfterScenario]` hook; the redundant `WithCancellation` is gone.

- Decisions: the orchestrator pinned these before the build. The real `run` command is AJ-054's, so the five scenarios run through the stand-in `PreflightRunCommand` and `RunCommandStandIn` with the real preflight, catalog client and provider pipeline, precedent AJ-052 and AJ-055. The design's composite `CheckAsync(tiers, dryRun)` and `DryRunService` stay with AJ-054. The ID lines are printed by the command through `ConsoleReporter.ReportModelNotFound`, because `ErrorLine` folds an exception message into one line. The judge model check is skipped outside dry-run when the judge key is missing (that path already warned under AJ-052 criterion 5). Provider-returned IDs are redacted and then echoed before they enter a message (the carry-over from the AJ-049 and AJ-052 gates); matching is done on echoed IDs on both sides, so a long ID or one with a control character still matches itself. `ModelIdListFormatter.Format` keeps at most 20 IDs, places every ID containing nemotron or qwen (case-insensitive) first, keeps provider order otherwise, and keeps the total count. The check stops at the first failing tier. The EDGE-008 tests live in a nested class so that the AJ-052 class trait does not tag them. Fix round decisions: matching is exact on the IDs as the provider wrote them and echoing is for display only, so two IDs sharing their first 64 characters or differing in a control character stay apart (the round-1 code review measured the collision with two 68-character GGUF tags); the key never leaves `ProviderClientFactory` (`CreateModelsRequest`); the preflight GET is capped at 30 s because a models listing is small and `--tier all` would otherwise wait up to 300 s per tier; the header count comes from the distinct raw IDs while the shown list is distinct after echo, so the header can read `first 4 of 5` when two raw IDs display the same (accepted, cosmetic, noted by the spec and security reviewers); a missing ID and a listed ID that share their first 64 characters display the same cut text in the error line and the list while the exit code is right (accepted; a longer echo length for model IDs is a possible later change); a non-2xx answer from `/models`, including 401, exits 5, not 3 (the spec does not cover it; recorded).

The catalog client depends on the concrete `ProviderClientFactory`. Criterion 5 (the Ollama hint) is M4 and is not implemented here. The builder briefly copied `DryRunTests.cs` one level above the repository through a wrong relative path and removed the copy at once (the orchestrator verified that git status is clean of it). ### AJ-056 Orchestrate a tier run over tasks, repeats and modes (REQ-017, REQ-013, EDGE-005)

- Shipped (builder 12:28Z to 13:07Z, tests written first; battery 1 13:09:23Z to 13:11:27Z): replaced the `RunOrchestrator` placeholder with the production orchestrator. Per selected tier it builds the `MeteringServices` (`SampledEnergyMeter` over the GPU opened again at `Gpu:Index` for `local` and `cloudgpu`, `UnmeteredEnergyMeter` for `nvidia` and `nebius`; the `IMeterGate` chosen by `--mode`; one in-memory sink per tier run, forwarded to `IRunListener`), with `ICostCalculator`, `AgentJoulesInstruments` and the host `TimeProvider`. A measured tier runs the start sequence (GPU port, meter start, warm-up once per distinct model ID, idle baseline for `--baseline-seconds` with `Baseline:MaxCoefficientOfVariation`, the EDGE-005 warning through `ConsoleReporter.ReportBaseline` on stderr). Then every task in file order and every repeat 1..n runs with fresh agents (`AgentFactory.CreateAll`) and a fresh workflow (`AgentJoulesWorkflowFactory.RunAsync`), one `running` checkpoint after each (task, repeat) and a final `completed` write; the meter and the GPU source are disposed in a `finally`. Shutdown (Ctrl+C, SIGTERM, SIGHUP, `ApplicationStopping`): each `cancelled` reading of the interrupted (task, repeat) is copied as `incomplete`, `run.status` becomes `incomplete`, the checkpoint is written with a fresh 10 s wall-clock token, then the cancellation is rethrown and the command exits 5. A shutdown before the first step still writes an incomplete file holding the probe. A `StepFailedException` propagates (exit 5). `RunAssembler` builds the `RunDocument` (run id, tasks, mode, repeat, `startUtc`, tool version and commit, environment, models, totals with labels, checkpoints that carry the totals so far). `ConsoleRunListener` (the Cli implementation of `IRunListener`) prints one line per finished step and the `results:` and `totals:` lines per tier through `ConsoleReporter`. `ReportSentences.ConcurrentOverlap` holds the fixed concurrent-overlap sentence. Seams: `IJudgeService` with `NoOpJudgeService`, `ILocalRuntime` with `LocalRuntimeInfo` and `NoOpLocalRuntime`. The run command's service hook registers the meter gate, the agent factory and `AgentJoulesInstruments` beside the telemetry; `RunPlan` gained `StartUtc` and `OutputDisplay`; `RunCommand` takes the start instant once for every tier's file name. Tests: the feature file `tests/AgentJoules.Specs/Features/Cli/AJ-056-run-orchestration.feature` (identical to the story block), its steps and context; `RunOrchestrationTests` with the nested classes `UnstableBaseline`, `Modes`, `Assembler`, `ConsoleLines` and `Seams`; `FakeTimeDriver` gained a step-cap parameter; `HoldingHttpMessageHandler` holds the n-th completion request for the shutdown scenario; the exit-5 row of `ExitCodeTests.RunCommandCodes` runs the production orchestrator; `FakeRunOrchestrator` stays the double of the AJ-052 to AJ-055 scenarios. Paths changed or new: 24. Fix round (2026-10-21, AJ-056): new files `RunListenerSink` (Workflow/Runs; keeps each reading first, catches every listener failure, holds the `_listenerLock` that serializes the listener calls), `RunLog` (a LoggerMessage class; Debug, exception type only), `TierKinds` (Core/Runs; IsMeasured, IsHosted, used by the orchestrator, the assembler, `PreflightService` and `StepReading`), `MetricLabelText` (Core/Labels; the labels `N/A` and `not-reported` for the JSON converter and the console lines, serialized strings unchanged) and `tests/AgentJoules.UnitTests/RunOrchestrationTests.Shutdown.cs`. `ResultsFileWriter.ThrowIfFileExists` is public and shared with the writer's first write. `HoldingHttpMessageHandler` bounds its hold at 60 s, and the shutdown tests cancel in a `finally`. Shutdown order: the incomplete checkpoint is written before the incomplete step lines are printed; its budget is min(10 s, 12 s minus the time since the signal, on the wall clock) and never below 1 s; a failed write (any exception except the cancellation) is logged at Debug and reported as one stderr warning line, and the command still exits 5; a tier that never started gets no file; a shutdown inside the local baseline wait writes a file with a null baseline, zero steps and every sum not-reported `no-steps`. Cost rule and early file refusal as in the design 3.4 amendment. Tests added: the measured-tier shutdown, the failed shutdown write, the between-tier cancellation, the listener-failure isolation and the published cancelled line; three judge-seam tests (splice back in place, skipped when the judge key is missing, wrong count throws `InvalidOperationException`); GPU release after a failed local step; a production-orchestrator leak proof (the fake key and the task prompt, echoed by the fake models list and the streamed answer, reach neither stdout, stderr nor any file under `results/`); the cost rows (mixed and all-priced) and the console `cost=N/A` row; the existing-file refusal on the fake clock; the REQ-013 trait on the serialized-totals test.

- Decisions: the concurrent energy totals of a measured tier are `not-reported` with the reason `union-not-computed` until AJ-047, instead of a computed union (the rationale is inferred from the story plan, not stated by the builder: AJ-047 owns the union branch and the `shared-window` flag); the shutdown write timeout runs on the wall clock, because a fake clock driver would otherwise cancel it; `baseline-unstable` is recorded in `baseline.flags`, because the flag describes the baseline; the console metrics exporter stays on after the summary lines, accepted for M2 (decision of the orchestrator); the judge seam is skipped when the judge key is missing, because outside dry-run a missing judge key only warns (AJ-052); the sampling-fallback line is printed once per command from the preflight result, not per tier. Fix-round decisions of the orchestrator: the incomplete checkpoint is written before the incomplete step lines, so the file is the record even when the console fails; a failed shutdown write keeps exit 5 and is reported on stderr (this replaces the earlier rule that it keeps its own exit code); the cost rule is the all-steps rule, so one unpriced step makes every currency N/A with `missing-step-costs`; the existing-file refusal runs when the writers are built, before the model check and before any completion call; the console metrics interval of 10 s is accepted as the M2 behaviour. A failed shutdown write is logged at Debug with the exception type only and reported as one stderr line `Warning: the run file could not be written after the shutdown (<Type>); the last complete checkpoint was kept` (or `...; no checkpoint of this tier was written` when no checkpoint of the tier exists; nits round), and the command still exits 5 (fix round, security MEDIUM-1; this replaced the builder's first choice of letting the write failure set the exit code). Nits round (code review round 2, verified by battery 3 only): the baseline-shutdown test holds the fake-clock driver until the cancellation callbacks ran (FakeTimeDriver beforeStep hook); one RunListenerSink per tier with a single failure flag carries every listener call; the warning line says whether a checkpoint of the tier exists.

No initial `running` write before the first (task, repeat) (design 3.4 step 2 lists one; criterion 2 governs). `ILocalRuntime.WarmUpAsync` returns `Task<TimeSpan?>` so the no-op yields null (`modelLoadSeconds` null, never 0), a deviation from the design signature `Task<TimeSpan>`. `baseline-unstable` is in `baseline.flags`, not `run.flags` (criterion 9). `RunAssembler`, `IRunListener`, `ConsoleRunListener`, `LocalRuntimeInfo`, `NoOpLocalRuntime`, `NoOpJudgeService`, `HoldingHttpMessageHandler` and `RunOrchestrationFixtures` are not in the task table. The builder's name for the `FakeTimeDriver` parameter is `maxStep`; the file uses `maxSteps`.

### M0 review2: suggestions, question and nitpicks of the final full-depth review (REQ-001, REQ-029)

- Shipped on branch milestone/M0-review2: `SecretRedactor.TrimEnds` returns a slice of the original string, decoding runes from both ends and keeping any invalid sequence (a lone surrogate) as visible, so the ends-trimmed form masks a raw echo of a malformed configured value; `RemoveInvisibleCharacters` drops invalid sequences for the clean form; `Register` registers the whole value and each of its CR- or LF-separated lines in up to three forms each (ends-trimmed, clean, rune-normalised with U+FFFD for an echo that passed through UTF-8), after the security gate showed that rejecting a multi-line value whole left a key on one of its lines unmasked and the code-review gate showed that per-line registration alone dropped short lines and newline-stripped echoes; the `RedactingTextWriter` summary is reflowed to 120 columns and states that a leaveOpen writer stays usable after Dispose and drains on every later Dispose (the AJ-034 console wrappers may receive output after the first drain and before the ProcessExit drain) while an owning writer throws; the generic Reqnroll step "the result is ..." asserts exact equality only; `tests/AgentJoules.Specs/Features/Configuration/.gitkeep` deleted because the folder holds a feature file; fifteen tests added over three gate rounds, two of them theories over the seven line terminators (61 cases on Spec=REQ-029).
